<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Namibia" title="Human rights in Namibia">Human rights in Namibia</a></div>
<p>Namibia is considered one of the most free and democratic countries in Africa,<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> with a government that maintains and protects basic human rights and freedoms. However, significant issues include - Government Corruption and Prison Overcrowding. Also, refugees are not permitted free movement.<sup id="cite_ref-USDOS_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDOS-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The law is not enforced,<sup id="cite_ref-:4_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> but homosexual acts are illegal in Namibia.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Discrimination, as well as intolerance, against <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Namibia" title="LGBT rights in Namibia">LGBT people</a> is widespread.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Some Namibian government officials and high-profile figures, such as Namibia's <a href="/wiki/Ombudsman" title="Ombudsman">Ombudsman</a> John Walters and First Lady <a href="/wiki/Monica_Geingos" title="Monica Geingos">Monica Geingos</a>, have called for <a href="/wiki/Sodomy" title="Sodomy">sodomy</a> and homosexuality to be decriminalised and are in favour of <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights" class="mw-redirect" title="LGBT rights">LGBT rights</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:4_108-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 2018, it was reported that 32% of women aged 15–49 have experienced <a href="/wiki/Violence_against_women" title="Violence against women">violence</a> and <a href="/wiki/Domestic_abuse" class="mw-redirect" title="Domestic abuse">domestic abuse</a> from their spouses/partners and 29.5% of men believe that physical abuse towards their wife/partner is acceptable.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> The Namibian constitution guarantees the rights, freedoms and equal treatment of women in Namibia<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> and SWAPO, the ruling party in Namibia, has adopted a "zebra system", which ensures a fair balance of both genders in government and equal representation of women in the Namibian government.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-USDOS_107-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDOS-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Namibia" title="Economy of Namibia">Economy of Namibia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg/220px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="155" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg/330px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/41/GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg/440px-GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="850" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GDP_per_capita_development_in_Southern_Africa.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Historical GDP per capita development in southern African countries, since 1950</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Downtown_Windhoek,_Independence_Avenue.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg/220px-Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg/330px-Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg/440px-Downtown_Windhoek%2C_Independence_Avenue.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="667" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Downtown_Windhoek,_Independence_Avenue.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Downtown <a href="/wiki/Windhoek" title="Windhoek">Windhoek</a></div></div></div>
<p>Namibia's economy is tied closely to <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_South_Africa" title="Economy of South Africa">South Africa</a>'s due to their shared history.<sup id="cite_ref-Intelligence1_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Intelligence1-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Department1_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Department1-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> The largest economic sectors are mining (10.4% of the gross domestic product in 2009), agriculture (5.0%), manufacturing (13.5%), and tourism (14.5%).<sup id="cite_ref-usds_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-usds-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Namibia has a highly developed banking sector with modern infrastructures, such as online banking and cellphone banking. The <a href="/wiki/Bank_of_Namibia" title="Bank of Namibia">Bank of Namibia</a> (BoN) is the central bank of Namibia responsible for performing all other functions ordinarily performed by a central bank. There are five BoN authorised commercial banks in Namibia: Bank Windhoek, First National Bank, Nedbank, Standard Bank and Small and Medium Enterprises Bank.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Namibia's economy is characterised by a divide between the formal and the informal economies, which is in part aggravated by the legacy of apartheid spatial planning.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to the Namibia Labour Force Survey Report 2012, conducted by the <a href="/wiki/Namibia_Statistics_Agency" title="Namibia Statistics Agency">Namibia Statistics Agency</a>, the country's unemployment rate is 27.4%.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> "Strict unemployment" (people actively seeking a full-time job) stood at 20.2% in 2000, 21.9% in 2004 and spiralled to 29.4% in 2008. Under a broader definition (including people who have given up searching for employment) unemployment rose to 36.7% in 2004. This estimate considers people in the <a href="/wiki/Informal_sector" class="mw-redirect" title="Informal sector">informal economy</a> as employed. Labour and Social Welfare Minister <a href="/wiki/Immanuel_Ngatjizeko" title="Immanuel Ngatjizeko">Immanuel Ngatjizeko</a> praised the 2008 study as "by far superior in scope and quality to any that has been available previously",<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> but its methodology has also received criticism.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2004 a labour act was passed to protect people from job discrimination stemming from pregnancy and HIV/AIDS status. In early 2010 the Government <a href="/wiki/Tender_board" title="Tender board">tender board</a> announced that "henceforth 100 per cent of all unskilled and semi-skilled labour must be sourced, without exception, from within Namibia".<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2013, global business and financial news provider, <a href="/wiki/Bloomberg_L.P." title="Bloomberg L.P.">Bloomberg</a>, named Namibia the top emerging market economy in Africa and the 13th best in the world. Only four African countries made the Top 20 Emerging Markets list in the March 2013 issue of Bloomberg Markets magazine, and Namibia was rated ahead of <a href="/wiki/Morocco" title="Morocco">Morocco</a> (19th), South Africa (15th), and <a href="/wiki/Zambia" title="Zambia">Zambia</a> (14th). Worldwide, Namibia also fared better than Hungary, Brazil, and Mexico. Bloomberg Markets magazine ranked the top 20 based on more than a dozen criteria. The data came from Bloomberg's own financial-market statistics, IMF forecasts and the World Bank. The countries were also rated on areas of particular interest to foreign investors: the ease of doing business, the perceived level of corruption and economic freedom. To attract foreign investment, the government has made improvement in reducing red tape resulted from excessive government regulations, making Namibia one of the least bureaucratic places to do business in the region. Facilitation payments are occasionally demanded by customs due to cumbersome and costly customs procedures.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Namibia is also classified as an Upper Middle Income country by the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, and ranks 87th out of 185 economies in terms of ease of doing business.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cost_of_living_in_Namibia" title="Cost of living in Namibia">cost of living in Namibia</a> is relatively high because most goods, including cereals, need to be imported. Its capital city, Windhoek, is the 150th most expensive place in the world for expatriates to live.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Taxation_in_Namibia" title="Taxation in Namibia">Taxation in Namibia</a> includes personal income tax, which is applicable to the total taxable income of an individual. All individuals are taxed at progressive marginal rates over a series of income brackets. The value-added tax (VAT) is applicable to most of the commodities and services.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay,_2005.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg/220px-Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg/330px-Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg/440px-Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay%2C_2005.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sandstorm_while_driving_from_Swakopmund_to_Walfish_Bay,_2005.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/B2_road_(Namibia)" title="B2 road (Namibia)">B2</a> between <a href="/wiki/Swakopmund" title="Swakopmund">Swakopmund</a> and <a href="/wiki/Walvis_Bay" title="Walvis Bay">Walvis Bay</a>, Namibia</div></div></div>
<p>Despite the remote nature of much of the country, Namibia has seaports, airports, highways, and <a href="/wiki/Rail_transport_in_Namibia" title="Rail transport in Namibia">railways</a> (narrow-gauge). It seeks to become a regional transportation hub; it has an important seaport and several landlocked neighbours. The Central Plateau already serves as a <a href="/wiki/Transportation_corridor" class="mw-redirect" title="Transportation corridor">transportation corridor</a> from the more densely populated north to South Africa, the source of four-fifths of Namibia's imports.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Almanac_2004_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Almanac_2004-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Income_disparity">Income disparity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Income disparity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Namibia is a country with a substantial income disparity. The data indicates that the current income share held by the highest 10% is approximately 51.8%. An additional figure describes the poverty gap: people living on US$2 or less in the country are approximately 17.72% of the population.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Agriculture_in_Namibia" title="Agriculture in Namibia">Agriculture in Namibia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Schildburgsdorf.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Schildburgsdorf.jpg/220px-Schildburgsdorf.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="113" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/13/Schildburgsdorf.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="326" data-file-height="167" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Schildburgsdorf.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Welcoming sign of the <i>Burgsdorf</i> farm in <a href="/wiki/Hardap_Region" title="Hardap Region">Hardap</a></div></div></div>
<p>About half of the population depends on agriculture (largely <a href="/wiki/Subsistence_agriculture" title="Subsistence agriculture">subsistence agriculture</a>) for its livelihood, but Namibia must still import some of its food. Although per capita GDP is five times the per capita GDP of Africa's poorest countries, the majority of Namibia's people live in rural areas and have a subsistence way of life. Namibia has one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_income_equality" title="List of countries by income equality">highest rates of income inequality</a> in the world, due in part to the fact that there is an urban economy and a more rural cashless economy. The inequality figures thus take into account people who do not actually rely on the formal economy for their survival. Although arable land accounts for &lt;1% of Namibia, (about .97%), nearly half of the population is employed in agriculture.<sup id="cite_ref-World_Almanac_2004_128-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-World_Almanac_2004-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>About 4,000 commercial farmers own almost half of Namibia's arable land.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>  The United Kingdom offered about $180,000 in 2004 to help finance Namibia's <a href="/wiki/Land_reform" title="Land reform">land reform</a> process, as Namibia plans to start expropriating land from white farmers to resettle landless black Namibians.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Germany has offered €1.1bn in 2021 over 30 years in reparations for the genocides in the early 20th century but the money will go towards infrastructure, healthcare and training programmes not land reform.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>An agreement has been reached on the <a href="/wiki/Privatisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Privatisation">privatisation</a> of several more enterprises in coming years, with hopes that this will stimulate much needed foreign investment, but reinvestment of environmentally derived capital has hobbled Namibian per capita income.<sup id="cite_ref-Lange_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lange-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> One of the fastest growing areas of economic development in Namibia is the growth of <a href="/wiki/Communal_Wildlife_Conservancies_in_Namibia" title="Communal Wildlife Conservancies in Namibia">wildlife conservancies</a>. These are particularly important to the rural, generally unemployed, population.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mining_and_electricity">Mining and electricity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Mining and electricity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mining_in_Namibia" title="Mining in Namibia">Mining in Namibia</a></div>
<p>Providing 25% of Namibia's revenue, mining is the single most important contributor to the economy.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Namibia is the fourth largest exporter of non-fuel minerals in Africa and was the world's fourth largest producer of <a href="/wiki/Uranium" title="Uranium">uranium</a>. There have been significant investment in <a href="/wiki/Uranium_mining" title="Uranium mining">uranium mining</a> and Namibia planned to become the largest exporter of uranium by 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> However, as of 2019 Namibia continued to produce 750 tons Uranium annually making it a smaller than average exporter in the competitive world market.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>  Rich <a href="/wiki/Alluvium" title="Alluvium">alluvial</a> diamond deposits make Namibia a primary source for gem-quality diamonds.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> While Namibia is known predominantly for its gem diamond and uranium deposits, a number of other minerals are extracted industrially such as <a href="/wiki/Lead" title="Lead">lead</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tungsten" title="Tungsten">tungsten</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gold" title="Gold">gold</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tin" title="Tin">tin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fluorspar" class="mw-redirect" title="Fluorspar">fluorspar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manganese" title="Manganese">manganese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marble" title="Marble">marble</a>, <a href="/wiki/Copper" title="Copper">copper</a> and <a href="/wiki/Zinc" title="Zinc">zinc</a>. There are offshore gas deposits in the Atlantic Ocean that are planned to be extracted in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-usds_117-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-usds-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> According to "The Diamond Investigation", a book about the global diamond market, from 1978, <a href="/wiki/De_Beers" title="De Beers">De Beers</a>, the largest diamond company, bought most of the Namibian diamonds, and would continue to do so, because "whatever government eventually comes to power they will need this revenue to survive".<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Domestic supply voltage is 220 V AC. Electricity is generated mainly by thermal and hydroelectric power plants. Non-conventional methods of electricity generation also play some role. Encouraged by the rich uranium deposits, in 2010 the Namibian government planned to erect its first nuclear power station by 2018. Uranium enrichment was also envisaged to take place locally.<sup id="cite_ref-Saveorsink_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Saveorsink-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Diamonds">Diamonds</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Diamonds">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Although much of the world's diamond supply comes from what have been called African <a href="/wiki/Blood_diamonds" class="mw-redirect" title="Blood diamonds">blood diamonds</a>, Namibia has managed to develop a diamond mining industry largely free of the kinds of conflict, extortion, and murder that have plagued many other African nations with diamond mines. This has been attributed to political dynamics, economic institutions, grievances, political geography, and the effects of neighbourhoods, and is the result of a joint agreement between the government and <a href="/wiki/De_Beers" title="De Beers">De Beers</a> that has led to a taxable base, strengthening state institutions.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Oil_and_natural_gas">Oil and natural gas</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Oil and natural gas">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Estimates updated in 2022 suggest that two exploration wells in the offshore Orange Basin could hold 2 and 3 billion barrels of oil, respectively. The expected revenue could transform Namibia's domestic economy and facilitate sustainable development goals.<sup id="cite_ref-bra1_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bra1-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Namibia" title="Tourism in Namibia">Tourism in Namibia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Equus_burchelli_4.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Equus_burchelli_4.jpg/220px-Equus_burchelli_4.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Equus_burchelli_4.jpg/330px-Equus_burchelli_4.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Equus_burchelli_4.jpg/440px-Equus_burchelli_4.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2742" data-file-height="2056" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Equus_burchelli_4.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An example of Namibian wildlife, the <a href="/wiki/Plains_zebra" title="Plains zebra">plains zebra</a>, is one focus of tourism.</div></div></div>
<p>Tourism is a major contributor (14.5%) to Namibia's GDP, creating tens of thousands of jobs (18.2% of all employment) directly or indirectly and servicing over a million tourists per year.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The country is a prime destination in Africa and is known for <a href="/wiki/Ecotourism" title="Ecotourism">ecotourism</a>, which features <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_Namibia" title="Wildlife of Namibia">Namibia's extensive wildlife</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-goodshape_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-goodshape-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are many lodges and reserves to accommodate ecotourists. Sport and <a href="/wiki/Trophy_hunting" title="Trophy hunting">trophy hunting</a> is also a large and growing component of the Namibian economy, accounting for 14% of total tourism in the year 2000, or 19.6&#160;million U.S. dollars, with Namibia boasting numerous species sought after by international sport hunters.<sup id="cite_ref-Humavindu_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Humavindu-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition, extreme sports such as <a href="/wiki/Sandboarding" title="Sandboarding">sandboarding</a>, <a href="/wiki/Skydiving" class="mw-redirect" title="Skydiving">skydiving</a> and 4x4ing have become popular, and many cities have companies that provide tours.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2014)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The most visited places include the capital city of <a href="/wiki/Windhoek" title="Windhoek">Windhoek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Caprivi_Strip" title="Caprivi Strip">Caprivi Strip</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fish_River_Canyon" title="Fish River Canyon">Fish River Canyon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sossusvlei" title="Sossusvlei">Sossusvlei</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Skeleton_Coast" title="Skeleton Coast">Skeleton Coast</a> Park, <a href="/wiki/Sesriem" title="Sesriem">Sesriem</a>, <a href="/wiki/Etosha_Pan" title="Etosha Pan">Etosha Pan</a> and the coastal towns of <a href="/wiki/Swakopmund" title="Swakopmund">Swakopmund</a>, <a href="/wiki/Walvis_Bay" title="Walvis Bay">Walvis Bay</a> and <a href="/wiki/L%C3%BCderitz" title="Lüderitz">Lüderitz</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Windhoek plays a very important role in Namibia's tourism due to its central location and close proximity to <a href="/wiki/Hosea_Kutako_International_Airport" title="Hosea Kutako International Airport">Hosea Kutako International Airport</a>. According to The Namibia Tourism Exit Survey, which was produced by the <a href="/wiki/Millennium_Challenge_Corporation" title="Millennium Challenge Corporation">Millennium Challenge Corporation</a> for the Namibian Directorate of Tourism, 56% of all tourists visiting Namibia in 2012–13 visited Windhoek.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> Many of Namibia's tourism related parastatals and governing bodies such as Namibia Wildlife Resorts and the <a href="/wiki/Namibia_Tourism_Board" title="Namibia Tourism Board">Namibia Tourism Board</a> as well as Namibia's tourism-related <a href="/wiki/Trade_association" title="Trade association">trade associations</a> such as the <a href="/wiki/Hospitality_Association_of_Namibia" title="Hospitality Association of Namibia">Hospitality Association of Namibia</a> are headquartered in Windhoek.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> There are also a number of notable hotels in Windhoek, such as <a href="/wiki/Windhoek_Country_Club_Resort" title="Windhoek Country Club Resort">Windhoek Country Club Resort</a>, and some international hotel chains, such as <a href="/wiki/Hilton_Hotels_%26_Resorts" title="Hilton Hotels &amp; Resorts">Hilton Hotels and Resorts</a>.
</p><p>Namibia's primary tourism related governing body, the <a href="/wiki/Namibia_Tourism_Board" title="Namibia Tourism Board">Namibia Tourism Board</a> (NTB), was established by an Act of Parliament: the <i>Namibia Tourism Board Act, 2000</i> (Act 21 of 2000). Its primary objectives are to regulate the tourism industry and to market Namibia as a tourist destination.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> There are also a number of <a href="/wiki/Trade_association" title="Trade association">trade associations</a> that represent the tourism sector in Namibia, such as the Federation of Namibia Tourism Associations (the umbrella body for all tourism associations in Namibia), the <a href="/wiki/Hospitality_Association_of_Namibia" title="Hospitality Association of Namibia">Hospitality Association of Namibia</a>, the Association of Namibian Travel Agents, Car Rental Association of Namibia and the Tour and Safari Association of Namibia.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Water_supply_and_sanitation">Water supply and sanitation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Water supply and sanitation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Namibia" title="Water supply and sanitation in Namibia">Water supply and sanitation in Namibia</a></div>
<p>The only bulk water supplier in Namibia is <a href="/wiki/NamWater" title="NamWater">NamWater</a>, which sells it to the respective municipalities which in turn deliver it through their reticulation networks.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> In rural areas, the Directorate of Rural Water Supply in the Ministry of Agriculture, Water and Forestry is in charge of drinking water supply.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">UN</a> evaluated in 2011 that Namibia has improved its water access network significantly since independence in 1990. A large part of the population can not, however, make use of these resources due to the prohibitively high consumption cost and the long distance between residences and water points in rural areas.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_149-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> As a result, many Namibians prefer the traditional wells over the available water points far away.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Compared to the efforts made to improve access to safe water, Namibia is lagging behind in the provision of adequate sanitation.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> This includes 298 schools that have no toilet facilities.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Over 50% of child deaths are related to lack of water, sanitation, or hygiene; 23% are due to diarrhoea alone. The UN has identified a "sanitation crisis" in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_150-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Apart from residences for upper and middle class households, sanitation is insufficient in most residential areas. Private flush toilets are too expensive for virtually all residents in <a href="/wiki/Township" title="Township">townships</a> due to their water consumption and installation cost. As a result, access to <a href="/wiki/Improved_sanitation" title="Improved sanitation">improved sanitation</a> has not increased much since independence: in Namibia's rural areas 13% of the population had more than basic sanitation, up from 8% in 1990. Many of Namibia's inhabitants have to resort to "flying toilets", plastic bags to defecate into, which after use are flung into the bush.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> The use of open areas close to residential land for urination and defecation is very common<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> and has been identified as a major <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Namibia" title="Health in Namibia">health hazard</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-152">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Namibia" title="Demographics of Namibia">Demographics of Namibia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Namibia_Population_Density_(2011).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg/330px-Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="330" height="294" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg/495px-Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg/660px-Namibia_Population_Density_%282011%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="836" data-file-height="744" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Namibia_Population_Density_(2011).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Population density in Namibia by regions (census 2011)</div></div></div>
<p>Namibia has the second-<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_density" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by population density">lowest population density</a> of any sovereign country, after <a href="/wiki/Mongolia" title="Mongolia">Mongolia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-unpop_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unpop-155">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup> In 2017 there were on average 3.08 people per km<sup>2</sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> in 2015 was 3.47 children per woman according to the UN.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The majority of the Namibian population is of <a href="/wiki/Bantu_peoples" title="Bantu peoples">Bantu</a>-speaking origin—mostly of the <a href="/wiki/Ovambo_people" title="Ovambo people">Ovambo</a> ethnicity, which forms about half of the population—residing mainly in the north of the country, although many are now resident in towns throughout Namibia. Other ethnic groups are the <a href="/wiki/Herero_people" title="Herero people">Herero</a> and <a href="/wiki/Himba_people" title="Himba people">Himba people</a>, who speak a similar language, and the <a href="/wiki/Damara_(people)" class="mw-redirect" title="Damara (people)">Damara</a>, who, like the <a href="/wiki/Nama_people" title="Nama people">Nama</a>, speak <a href="/wiki/Khoekhoe_language" title="Khoekhoe language">Khoekhoe</a>.
</p><p>In addition to the Bantu majority, there are large groups of <a href="/wiki/Khoisan" title="Khoisan">Khoisan</a> (such as Nama and <a href="/wiki/San_people" title="San people">San</a>), who are descendants of the original inhabitants of Southern Africa. The country also contains some <a href="/wiki/Angolans_in_Namibia" title="Angolans in Namibia">descendants of refugees from Angola</a>. There are also two smaller groups of people with mixed racial origins, called "<a href="/wiki/Coloured_people_in_Namibia" title="Coloured people in Namibia">Coloureds</a>" and "<a href="/wiki/Baster" title="Baster">Basters</a>", who together make up 8.0% (with the Coloureds outnumbering the Basters two to one). There is a substantial <a href="/wiki/Chinese_people_in_Namibia" title="Chinese people in Namibia">Chinese minority in Namibia</a>; it stood at 40,000 in 2006.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG/220px-Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG/330px-Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG/440px-Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Himba_Woman_and_Family.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Himba_people" title="Himba people">Himba people</a> in northern Namibia</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/White_African" class="mw-redirect" title="White African">Whites</a> (being mainly of <a href="/wiki/Afrikaner_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Afrikaner people">Afrikaner</a>, German, British and <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_diaspora" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese diaspora">Portuguese</a> origin) make up between 4.0 and 7.0% of the population. Although their proportion of the population decreased after <a href="/wiki/Independence_of_Namibia" class="mw-redirect" title="Independence of Namibia">independence</a> due to emigration and lower birth rates, they still form the second-largest population of <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Europe" title="Ethnic groups in Europe">European ancestry</a>, both in terms of percentage and actual numbers, in <a href="/wiki/Sub-Saharan_Africa" title="Sub-Saharan Africa">Sub-Saharan Africa</a> (after South Africa).<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> The majority of <a href="/wiki/White_Namibians" title="White Namibians">Namibian whites</a> and nearly all those who are of <a href="/wiki/Basters" class="mw-redirect" title="Basters">mixed race</a> speak <a href="/wiki/Afrikaans" title="Afrikaans">Afrikaans</a> and share similar origins, culture, and religion as the white and coloured populations of South Africa. A large minority of whites (around 30,000) trace their family origins back to the <a href="/wiki/German_Namibians" title="German Namibians">German</a> settlers who colonised Namibia prior to the South African invasion during the First World War, and they maintain German cultural and educational institutions. Nearly all Portuguese settlers came to the country from the former <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_West_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese West Africa">Portuguese colony</a> of Angola.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> The 1960 census reported 526,004 persons in what was then South West Africa, including 73,464 whites (14%).<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Toutrek.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Toutrek.jpg/220px-Toutrek.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Toutrek.jpg/330px-Toutrek.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Toutrek.jpg/440px-Toutrek.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2304" data-file-height="1728" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Toutrek.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Afrikaner" class="mw-redirect" title="Afrikaner">Afrikaner</a> children in Namibia</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Censuses">Censuses</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Censuses">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Namibia conducts a census every ten years. After independence the first Population and Housing Census was carried out in 1991; further rounds followed in 2001 and 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-2001Census_161-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2001Census-161">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> The data collection method is to count every person resident in Namibia on the census reference night, wherever they happen to be. This is called the <i>de facto</i> method.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> For enumeration purposes the country is demarcated into 4,042 <i>enumeration areas</i>. These areas do not overlap with constituency boundaries to get reliable data for election purposes as well.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2011 Population and Housing Census counted 2,113,077 inhabitants. Between 2001 and 2011 the annual population growth was 1.4%, down from 2.6% in the previous ten-year period.<sup id="cite_ref-snapshot_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-snapshot-164">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Urban_settlements">Urban settlements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Urban settlements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Namibia" title="List of cities and towns in Namibia">List of cities and towns in Namibia</a></div>
<p>Namibia has 13 cities, governed by municipalities and 26 towns, governed by town councils.<sup id="cite_ref-EW15_165-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EW15-165">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> The capital Windhoek is by far the largest urban settlement in Namibia.
</p>
<div>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Namibia" title="Religion in Namibia">Religion in Namibia</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg/220px-Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg/330px-Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg/440px-Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5526" data-file-height="3684" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Swakopmund_ev-luth_Kirche_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Lutheran church in <a href="/wiki/Swakopmund" title="Swakopmund">Swakopmund</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Christian community makes up 80%–90% of the population of Namibia, with at least 75% being <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a>, of which at least 50% are <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheran</a>. Lutherans are the largest religious group, a legacy of the German and <a href="/wiki/Finland" title="Finland">Finnish</a> missionary work during the country's colonial times. 10%–20% of the population hold <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples" title="Indigenous peoples">indigenous</a> beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_158-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-158">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Missionary activities during the second half of the 19th century resulted in many Namibians converting to Christianity. Today most Christians are <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a>, but there also are <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Methodism" title="Methodism">Methodist</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anglicanism" title="Anglicanism">Anglican</a>, <a href="/wiki/African_Methodist_Episcopal_Church" title="African Methodist Episcopal Church">African Methodist Episcopal</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Reformed_Church" title="Dutch Reformed Church">Dutch Reformed</a>, <a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">Latter-day Saints</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Namibia" title="Islam in Namibia">Islam in Namibia</a> is subscribed to by about 9,000 people,<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> many of them Nama.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> Namibia is home to a small <a href="/wiki/Judaism" title="Judaism">Jewish</a> community of about 100 people.<sup id="cite_ref-jewishvirtuallibrary1_170-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jewishvirtuallibrary1-170">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Namibia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Namibia" title="Languages of Namibia">Languages of Namibia</a></div>
